description: |-
  The defense industrial base of the future will be built by small, fast-moving teams working on some of the hardest problems in national security.

  Live from Washington, D.C., Y Combinator brings together founders, senior government officials, and military leaders for a day of conversations about rebuilding America’s defense industrial base, working with the government, and closing critical capability gaps across autonomy, munitions, space, secure communications, manufacturing, and more.

  Full event agenda here: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1RktUN1vOL4uzPYhwJcdsUvc1molLQhFaQApB_FIMzOk/edit?usp=sharing

[2:14:28] describe these as markets in the defense
[2:14:29] community that are not locked up. There

[2:14:33] any of these sectors. And so new
[2:14:35] interests are going to have an
[2:14:36] incredible ability to step in there. The
[2:14:38] other thing that's going to help is
[2:14:40] acquisition reform. We're trying to move
[2:14:42] from a program manager structure to a
[2:14:44] portfolio acquisition executive. Why is
[2:14:47] that important? Program manager has a
[2:14:49] very narrow task he has to do, a narrow
[2:14:51] capability has to field. His resources

[2:14:56] against that. And so when a startup

[2:15:05] capabilities and create opportunities
[2:15:06] for startups to get involved. And then

## [5:33:12] Johnny Doyle (ProvenMetal)

[5:33:13] There we go. Hey guys, my name is

[5:33:26] amount. So PCB stands for printed
[5:33:28] circuit board. It is the single most
[5:33:30] critical bottleneck across aerospace
[5:33:32] defense and robotics applications. And
[5:33:35] the existing American supply chain is
[5:33:37] really really bad. And so we are on a
[5:33:39] mission to fix that. And it's a really
[5:33:42] really tough problem to solve. So market
[5:33:44] context, the US in the year 2000
[5:33:46] manufactured 30% of the world's circuit
[5:33:48] boards. Now that number is 4%. This is
[5:33:51] not good because it is really really
[5:33:52] important. And so there's something
[5:33:55] weird happening in this industry where
[5:33:56] when you when you talk to us
[5:33:58] manufacturers, they say there is not
[5:34:00] enough demand. We are dramatically
[5:34:02] underutilized. And then you go to the
[5:34:04] customers, these hardware companies, and
[5:34:05] they say there's too much demand and not
[5:34:07] enough supply. And so there's something
[5:34:09] weird going on in this industry. And so
[5:34:12] when you break it down into what
[5:34:13] actually attributes the majority of the
[5:34:15] lead time for these circuit boards, the
[5:34:17] vast majority of it is not
[5:34:18] manufacturing. It is everything going on
[5:34:20] around the manufacturing. This is
[5:34:22] quoting, designer review, uh sourcing of
[5:34:25] all the components and the kitting
[5:34:26] processes or preparation for all those
[5:34:28] components before they before they go
[5:34:29] into a assembled circuit board. And so
[5:34:32] what we found is that if we partner with
[5:34:34] these existing manufacturers and
[5:34:36] automate these processes, we can
[5:34:37] immediately cut leons in half through
[5:34:39] automation. And so the first thing that
[5:34:41] we're doing is essentially automating
[5:34:44] the supply chain by by integrating with
[5:34:46] existing manufacturers. You can think of
[5:34:48] this like a network where every

[5:35:17] manufacturing itself needs to be
